srv  update_chat_: Parsing chat message: Quicksort is a highly efficient, **divide-and-conquer** sorting algorithm that works by selecting a 'pivot' element from the array and partitioning the other elements into two sub-arrays — one with elements **less than** the pivot and one with elements **greater than** the pivot — then recursively sorting the sub-arrays.

---

### 🧠 Core Idea
1. **Choose a pivot** (e.g., first, last, middle, or random element).
2. **Partition**: Rearrange the array so that:
   - Elements `< pivot` are on the left.
   - Elements `> pivot` are on the right.
   - Pivot ends up in its final sorted position.
3. **Recursively apply** steps 1–2 to the left and right sub-arrays.

---

### 📜 Algorithm (High-Level)
```
quicksort(A, low, high):
    if low < high:
        p = partition(A, low, high)   // partition and get pivot index
        quicksort(A, low, p - 1)      // sort left part
        quicksort(A, p + 1, high)     // sort right part
```

#### Partitioning (Lomuto scheme —

This solution includes:
1.  **Database**: PostgreSQL (via SQLAlchemy).
2.  **Authentication**: JWT (JSON Web Tokens) with Bearer tokens.
3.  **Data Models**: Pydantic schemas for validation and serialization.
4.  **CRUD**: Create, Read, Update, Delete endpoints.
5.  **Error Handling**: Custom exception handlers and standard HTTP exceptions.
6.  **Security**: Password hashing and user isolation (users can only see their own tasks).

### Project Structure
```text
task_manager_api/
├── main.py                 # App entry point, routes, and error handlers
├── models.py               # Database models (SQLAlchemy)
├── schemas.py              # Pydantic models
├── database.py             # DB connection and session management
├── auth.py                 # Password hashing and JWT logic
└── requirements.txt
```

---

### 1. Dependencies (`requirements.txt`)
```text
fastapi==0.109.0
uvicorn==0.27.0
sqlalchemy==2.0.25
psycopg2-binary==2.9.9
python-jose[cryptography]==3.3.0
passlib[bcrypt]==1.7.4
python-multipart==0.0.6
```

### 2. Database Setup (`database.py`)
We use SQLAlchemy with a connection to PostgreSQL. (For a real app, use environment variables for `DATABASE_URL`).

```python
# database.py
from sqlalchemy import create_engine
from sqlalchemy.ext.declarative import declarative_base
from sqlalchemy.orm import sessionmaker

# Replace with your actual DB URL
DATABASE_URL = "postgresql://user:password@localhost:5432/taskdb"

engine = create_engine(DATABASE_URL, echo=True)
SessionLocal = sessionmaker(autocommit=False, autoflush=False, bind=engine)

Base = declarative_base()

def get_db():
    db = SessionLocal()
    try:
        yield db
    finally:
        db.close()
```

---

### 3. Database Models (`models.py`)
We define the User and Task tables.

```python
# models.py
from sqlalchemy import Column, Integer, String, Boolean, ForeignKey
from sqlalchemy.orm import relationship
from database import Base

class User(Base):
    __tablename__ = "users"

    id = Column(Integer

### 2. Database Setup (`database.py`)

```python
# database.py
from sqlalchemy import create_engine
from sqlalchemy.ext.declarative import declarative_base
from sqlalchemy.orm import sessionmaker

# Replace with your actual DB URL
DATABASE_URL = "postgresql://user:password@localhost:5432/taskdb"

engine = create_engine(DATABASE_URL, echo=True)
SessionLocal = sessionmaker(autocommit=False, autoflush=False, bind=engine)

Base = declarative_base()

def get_db():
    db = SessionLocal()
    try:
        yield db
    finally:
        db.close()
```

---
